Revocable and Limited License
to Use Licensor’s Parking Lot/Land
1.
Defined Terms
(05/14/2024): This License to enter upon Licensor’s Property to use the Parking Lot on Licensor’s Parking
Lot/Land (“License Agreement”) is effective as of  05/14/2024 and governs Licensee’s (defined below) right
to [conduct emergency disaster relief activities solely related to furthering Licensee’s charitable mission
and] park its Vehicles (defined below) on the parking lot and/or land owned by the Licensor (“Parking Lot”),
which is more particularly described in and diagrammed on Exhibit A (attached hereto and incorporated
herein), pursuant to the terms and conditions set forth herein.
Owner and Licensor of the Parking Lot (“Licensor”): Navajo County, Arizona
Legal Name of the User of the Parking Lot (“Licensee”): The American National Red Cross, a nonprofit
corporation, a Federally chartered instrumentality of the United States, and a body corporate and politic
under the laws of the United States (36 U.S.C. §§ 300101-300111 (2007)).
Date Upon which the Licensee May Begin to Use the Parking Lot (“Start Date”): 05/14/2024.
Date Upon Which the Licensee Must Vacate the Parking Lot (“Expiration Date”): 05/14/2025. The
period beginning on the Start Date and ending on the Expiration Date is referred to herein as the “Term”.
Parking Lot Owner’ and Licensor’s Business Addresses: 100 Code Talkers Dr, Holbrook, AZ 86025
Licensee’s Notice Address: 100 Code Talkers Dr, Holbrook, AZ 86025
Complete Street Address of Licensor’s/Owner’s Property: 100 W Public Works Dr. Holbrook, AZ
86025
Street Address Where the Parking Lot is Located (if the same as above, insert Same):
Same_________________________________
Diagram of Licensed Area Within the Parking Lot (“Licensed Area”): See attached Exhibit A.
Maximum Number of Parking Spaces to be Used by Vehicles: _____2________
Maximum Number of Vehicles That May be Parked on the Parking Lot at any One Time:  ___2___
2.
Grant of License.  As of the Effective Date and on the conditions specified in this License Agreement,
Licensor grants to Licensee and its employees, a revocable license to: (a) enter upon and have reasonable
vehicular and pedestrian ingress and egress over and across the Parking Lot to and from the Licensed
Area; and (b) park up to the maximum number of Vehicles[ and conduct emergency disaster relief activities
as follows:] in the Licensed Area. Licensor shall have the sole but reasonable right of approval of all the
means and methods of such access. Licensee shall be permitted to have access to the Licensed Area from
the hours of _0001 Hrs. to 2400 Hrs. during the Term only for the purposes of parking private passenger
vehicles and/or trucks (each and collectively, “Vehicles”) [ and conduct emergency disaster relief activities
as follows: Vehicle and Trailer storage in the Parking Lot (collectively, the “Licensed Use”). At no time shall
Licensee have any right to bring onto the Property, Licensed Area and/or the Parking Lot or park or idle
more than the maximum number of Vehicles on any of Licensor’s Property or in other areas of the Parking
Lot other than as designated by Licensor and set forth on Exhibit A or conduct any activities on the Owner’s
Property, other than the Permitted Use, without Licensor’s prior consent.
3.
License Fee/Payment: None.
4.
AS-IS, WHERE IS, WITH ALL FAULTS CONDITION.  To the fullest extent permitted by applicable
law, Licensee’s vehicle and pedestrian access entry onto any the Property, parking of vehicles or
conducting  any other activities related to the Permitted Use shall be at the sole risk of Licensee. Licensee
understands, agrees, and accepts the Parking Lot and Property in its AS-IS, WHERE IS, WITH ALL
FAULTS CONDITION.
5.
Licensee’s Conduct.  Licensee’s Licensed Use shall not unreasonably disrupt, adversely affect or
interfere Licensor’s use of other areas of the Parking Lot or the Property.
6.
Preparation and Repair of Parking Lot.  Licensor is under no obligation whatsoever to prepare the
Property, Parking Lot or the Licensed Area for Licensee’s entry onto or use of same.  Licensee will repair
the Licensed Area in accordance with Licensor’s normal business practices to the extent such repairs are
caused by Licensee’s (or its employees’, agents’ or volunteers’) negligent acts or omissions.

<!-- page 310 -->
LOG-Parking-Lot-Agreement-V.1.0-2023-07-12
2
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
7.
Revocation of License; Removal.  Licensor or Licensee may revoke the permission granted by this
License Agreement, and therefore terminate this License Agreement, at any time and for any reason or for
no reason by giving Licensee not less than five  days’ prior written notice. On or before the Expiration Date
or effective date of any earlier termination of this License Agreement, as applicable, Licensee shall remove
all Vehicles and Licensee’s property from the Property. Licensee shall be responsible for repairing any
damage to the Licensed Area, the Property and/or the Parking Lot to the extent caused by Licensee (or its
employees or agents), casualty and reasonable wear and tear excepted. If Licensee fails to complete such
repairs within 30 days after the effective date of any termination or expiration of this License Agreement,
Licensor may make such repairs, and Licensee will reimburse Licensor its reasonable costs thereof within
30 days of Licensee’s receipt of Licensor’s invoice and reasonable, written documentation therefor.
8.
Indemnification; Limitation of Liability.  Licensee shall indemnify, defend and hold Licensor, its
parent, directors, officers and employees harmless from and against legal liability arising out of Licensee’s
negligent use of the Licensed Area, the Property and/or the Parking Lot.
9.
Insurance. Licensee shall carry insurance for Commercial General Liability and Auto Liability with the
following minimum limits: $1,000,000 occurrence and, and such insurance policies shall name The
American National Red Cross and its chapters, affiliates, successors and assigns as an “additional insured”
thereunder. Prior to entering onto Licensor’s Property, Licensee shall provide to Licensor proof (acceptable
to Licensor) of Licensee carrying and maintaining the insurance required by this Section 9.
10. Remedies.  If Licensee fails to cure its breach of any obligation under this License Agreement within
five (5) days after receiving Licensor’s written notice (including reasonable details thereof), Licensor shall
have the right to terminate this License Agreement and the license granted hereunder by providing written
notice to Licensee of its exercise of such right; provided, however, that such cure period shall be extended
for a reasonable period of time if the failure does not raise a life/safety issue and Licensee is diligently
undertaking good faith efforts to effect a cure.
11. Notice.  Notices shall be sent to Licensor and Licensee at the respective addresses of each party as
set forth in Section 1 of this License Agreement and shall be deemed to have been duly given: (a) on the
date of receipt by the addressee, if delivered by hand, with reliable proof of delivery; or (b) on the date of
receipt, if delivered by a recognized overnight delivery service that provides proof of delivery. Either party
may change its address for the purpose of notice hereunder by providing the other party with notice of the
new address by a means set forth in this Section 11.
12. Force Majeure.  Neither party hereunder shall be liable or responsible to the other party, nor be
deemed to have defaulted under or breached this License Agreement, for any failure or delay in fulfilling
or performing any term hereof this Lease, when and to the extent such party's  failure or delay (the
“Impacted Party”) is caused by or results from any of the following events that affect the Impacted Party
(each a “Force Majeure Event”): (a) act of God; (b) flood, fire, earthquake, epidemics, pandemics, or
explosion; (c) war, invasion, hostilities (whether war is declared or not), terrorist threats or acts, riot, or
other civil unrest; or (d) government order, law, or action, including, without limitation, quarantines, stay-at-
home orders, and other similar governmental orders.  The Impacted Party shall give notice to the other
party of the Force Majeure Event as soon as reasonably practicable after the occurrence thereof. The
Impacted Party shall commence performance of its obligations hereunder to the extent same is practical
under the circumstances. The Impacted Party shall use commercially reasonable efforts to resume
performing its obligations to the extent reasonably practical after the Force Majeure Event ceases.
13. Governing Law and Binding Effect.  This License Agreement shall be governed by and construed
under the laws of the state in which the Parking Lot is located. This License Agreement shall be binding
on the parties and their respective, successors, transferees and assigns.
14. Brokers/Consultants.   Licensee and Licensor, each for themselves, represents and warrants that no
broker was involved in this transaction. A party hereto shall indemnify the other party against any claims
of any broker or third-party who purports to have represented a party to this License Agreement.
15. Entire Agreement. This License Agreement contains all the terms and conditions to which the parties
have agreed and supersedes any and all prior understandings, agreements, promises, warranties and
representations, whether oral or written, relating to the subject matter of this License Agreement.
16. Counterparts.  This License Agreement may be executed in any number of counterparts, each of
which shall be deemed an original and all of which together shall comprise but one and the same
instrument.
17. Electronic Signatures.  Documents: (a) manually executed and scanned; or (b) signed with an
electronic signature, imprint or copy of a manual signature, and in either case, transmitted electronically,
shall be deemed originals for all purposes of this License Agreement and all matters related thereto, with
such electronic signatures having the same legal effect as original signatures.

Motion before the Board:
**Amendment No 1 to Delegation Agreement 06-073 with the Arizona Department of Environmental
Quality extending the expiration of the agreement until May 23, 2026
Background:
Navajo County Emergency Management was provided a Delegation Agreement from the Arizona
Department of Environmental Quality to provide Open Burn Permits to the residents of Navajo County
that are not within the jurisdiction of another agency. Citizens can find out where they can obtain a burn
permit by visiting www.navajocountyaz.gov/ready and search for their address on the map. All the fire
districts and department's boundaries are highlighted in different colors to show their response boundaries.

Board of Supervisors Regular
4. b.        
Meeting Date: 05/14/2024
 
Title:
Recognition of Snowflake All American Award Kaytlin LaDuke
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department:
Board of Supervisors
Presented By: Chairman Jason Whiting
Subject:
Presentation to Kaytlin LaDuke as the recipient of the Scholar All-America Award and the Character &
Leadership All-America Award
Background: 
Kaytlin's commitment to wrestling and academic excellence has not gone unnoticed. She now stands as a
proud recipient of two prestigious USMC/National Wrestling Coaches Association All-American awards:
The Scholar All-America Award and the Character & Leadership All-America Award. These honors
recognize her outstanding achievements both on and off the mat, showcasing her exceptional academic
prowess, unwavering character, and remarkable leadership skills.
Her journey in wrestling began as one of the first girls to venture into the Snowflake Girls Wrestling
program. Despite facing early setbacks due to injury in her debut 2022-2023 season, Kaytlin's
determination remained unshaken. In the 2023-2024 season, she has showcased her resilience and skill,
finishing with an impressive record of 35-5; 30 of those wins were by pin, setting a new standard of
excellence for herself and her team. This record ranks her 10th in Arizona Girls wrestling history for pins
in a single season.

Title:

Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration of a Franchise Agreement with Cactus State Utility Operating
Company LLC, for the provision of water services. 
Background:
Cactus State Utility Operating Company, LLC (“Cactus State”) recently acquired the High Country Pines
(High Country Pines) Water Company, Inc.’s water system.  Navajo County's Franchise Agreement with
High Country Pines expired in 2018. Therefore, Cactus State will need their own Franchise Agreement. 
Notice of public hearing had been published in the newspaper of record for 3 consecutive weeks prior to
this date. 
See the statute below for more information:
E. A board of supervisors, before granting any of the privileges authorized under this section, shall give
public notice of its intention to grant such
privileges by publishing notice in a newspaper of general circulation, published within the county, at least
once a week for three weeks before the
day set for consideration of such action. If, on or before such date, more than fifty percent of the qualified
electors of the county petition the board
of supervisors to deny the privilege, it shall do so, and any privilege granted against the petition is void.
 
Recommendation: 
Approve Franchise Agreement.

FRANCHISE AND LICENSE TO  
CACTUS STATE UTILITY OPERATING COMPANY, LLC.  
FOR 
THE PROVISION OF WATER SERVICES 
WHEREAS, the Cactus State Utility Operating Company, LLC, an Arizona public service 
corporation (herein called “Company”), has made application to the Board of Supervisors of Navajo 
County, Arizona (herein called “County”), for a non-exclusive franchise, license and right of way (the 
“Franchise”) with the right to construct, operate and maintain water lines, plant and systems, including 
mains, services, pumps, valves and valve boxes, meters, regulator stations and related equipment, 
facilities and appurtenances associated with the Franchisee's business of supplying water service to 
inhabitants of the County and others, and to the County, and for the passage, right-of-way and the right 
to occupy and use in any lawful and reasonable way during the life of this Franchise every and any 
and all County roads, highways, streets, bridges and other public places of County (other than State 
highways and public ways within the confines of any incorporated city or town) now existing or may 
be hereinafter extended (the “Premises”) for the service, use, effect and lawful purpose as herein stated. 
WHEREAS, said application has been considered and found to be reasonable and proper: 
NOW, THEREFORE, IT IS HEREBY ORDERED BY THE BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
OF NAVAJO COUNTY, ARIZONA: 
Section 1— Grant of Franchise 
County, subject to the provisions hereof, hereby grants to and vests in Company the Franchise 
with the right to operate and maintain water lines, plant and systems, including mains, services, 
pumps, valves and valve boxes, meters, regulator stations and related equipment, facilities and 
appurtenances associated with the Franchisee’s business of supplying water service and to sell, store, 
distribute, convey or otherwise establish, conduct, serve, supply or furnish the inhabitants of the 
County and others within the Franchise Area as depicted in Exhibit A attached hereto, and to the 
County whenever it may desire to contract therefore for water service; the Company is further granted 
passage, right-of-way and the right to occupy and use the Premises, as now existing or hereinafter 
extended, in any lawful and reasonable way during the life of this Franchise; provided, this grant shall 
not apply to any state highway or to any roads, highways, streets, avenue, alleys, bridges or other 
structures or public grounds within the confines of any incorporated city or town. 
Section 2 — Use of Premises and Relocation of Facilities 
The Company is hereby authorized, licensed and empowered to do any and all things necessary 
and proper to be done and performed in executing the powers and utilizing the privileges herein 
mentioned and granted by this Franchise, provided the same do not unreasonably conflict with pre-
existing water or other pipes, sewers or other underground installations, and that all work done in the 
Premises by the Company shall be done with the utmost diligence and the least practicable 
inconvenience to the public or individuals, and the Company shall within a reasonable time, restore 
the Premises excavated by it to its original condition as nearly as practicable, subject to the reasonable 
approval of the County. Franchisee shall bear all expenses, including damage and compensation, for 
any alteration of the direction, surface, grade or alignments of a public road bridge, sidewalk, 
driveway or other public way, including replacement of any plantings or landscaping made for the 
purpose of this franchise. The Company shall remove or relocate its facilities as and when required 
by the County to accommodate improvements within the Premises for the public benefit and any 
removal or relocation shall be made at the sole cost and expense of the Company, unless the 
Company can demonstrate that its facilities were lawfully installed prior to the dedication to or 
acquisition by the County of the property in question. All work and other actions as may be otherwise

<!-- page 331 -->
required or contemplated under this Franchise by the Company and/or its agents shall be in 
accordance with applicable federal and state laws, industry codes and County codes, policies and 
regulations. 
Section 3 — Indemnification 
The Company shall hold the County, its Board of Supervisors, officers and agents, harmless 
from any and all liability caused by the Company's negligence as a result of the construction, 
installation, or operation hereunder of the Company's facilities in the Premises. The rights of any 
persons claiming to be injured or damaged in any manner by the Company or by the exercise of any 
right or provision hereunder shall not be affected hereby. 
Section 4 — Insurance 
The Company agrees that at all times during the existence of this Franchise, that it will maintain 
in force, at its own expense, a general liability insurance program to adequately insure and/or protect 
the legal liability of the Company with respect to the installation, operation, and maintenance of its 
facilities, together with all the necessary and desirable appurtenances authorized by this Franchise, to 
occupy the Premises. Such insurance program will provide protection for bodily injury and property 
damage arising from the operation by the Company of its facilities. The policy limits or any insurance 
maintained in compliance with this section shall not limit the Company’s indemnification requirements 
under Section 3 of this Franchise. 
Section 5 — Applicable Rates and Charges 
The rates and charges to be charged by the Company for furnishing water service hereunder 
and the rules and regulations to be made and enforced by the Company for the conduct of its business 
shall be those from time to time on file and effective with the Arizona Corporation Commission 
applicable to such service. 
Section 6 — Assignment 
The Company shall have the right and privilege of assigning this Franchise and all rights and 
privileges granted herein, subject to approval by the Board of Supervisors which shall not be 
unreasonably denied. Whenever the word “Company” appears herein, it shall be construed as applying 
to its successors and assigns. This section shall not be deemed a warranty of assignability by the County. 
Section 7 — Approval and Acceptance of Franchise 
This Franchise shall be accepted by the Company in writing, which acceptance shall be 
evidenced by signature affixed below, and when so accepted, and after delivery to the County within 
sixty (60) days after the passage of this grant, this grant shall be a contract duly executed by and between 
the County and the Company. 
Section 8 — Independent Provisions 
If any section, paragraph, subdivision, clause, phrase or provision hereof shall be adjudged 
invalid or unconstitutional, the same shall not affect the validity hereof as a whole, or any part or 
provision other than the part so decided to be invalid or unconstitutional. 
Section 9 — Public Safety, Welfare and Convenience 
The County may, at any time hereafter, impose according to law such restrictions and 
limitations, additional to these incorporated herein, as to the use by the Company of such roads,

<!-- page 332 -->
highways, streets, avenues, alleys, bridges and other structures and public places as the Board deems 
best for the public safety and welfare and convenience. 
Section 10 — Coordination with Municipal Franchises 
This Franchise shall cease to confer upon the Company any rights or of any city or town when 
the latter privileges within the limits shall have incorporated and there shall be in effect its municipal 
franchise in favor of the Company for water service purposes. 
Section 11 — Term 
This Franchise shall continue in full force and effect for a period of twenty-five (25) years 
from its date of passage. All previous franchises granted to Franchisee by the Board of Supervisors, 
and any and all resolutions and parts thereof that may conflict with the terms hereof, are repealed. 
Section 12 - Ownership of Company Property 
All plant, system, facilities, works, and all other physical property installed or operated by the 
Company in accordance with the terms of this Franchise shall be and remain the property of the 
Company, and upon expiration of this Franchise or any extension or renewal thereof, the Company is 
hereby granted the right to enter upon the Premises for the purpose of removing any and all plant, 
system, facilities, works and other property of the Company, at any time within six months after 
termination of this Franchise or any extension or renewal thereof. 
Section 13 — Superseding Former Franchise 
After the passage of this grant and upon its acceptance by the Company all rights and privileges 
heretofore granted by the County by that certain franchise and license to the Company adopted by 
Resolution 92-93 shall be superseded by this grant and shall then be of no further force or effect. 
PASSED AND EFFECTIVE this _____ day of _____________, 2024

Title:
Proclamation for National Police Week
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By:
Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department:
Sheriff 
Presented By:
Sheriff David Clouse
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and approval of a Proclamation, proclaiming May 12 through May 18, 2024, as "National
Police Week" in Navajo County, Arizona.
Background:
In the year 1965, President Kennedy proclaimed May 15th as National Peace Officer Memorial Day, and
the calendar week in which it falls, National Police Week.  The members of the law enforcement agency
of the Navajo County Sheriff's Office play an important role in safeguarding the rights and freedoms of
the citizens of our community.  It is important that all citizens know and understand the problems, duties,
and responsibilities of their Sheriff's Office.  It is also important that the members of the Office recognize
their duty to serve the people by safeguarding life and property, protecting them against violence and/or
disorder, protecting the innocent against deception, and the weak against oppression or intimidation. 
Now, therefore, be it proclaimed.
Recommendation: 
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office recommends the designation and adoption of the week of May 12th
through May 18th, 2024 as National Police Week in Navajo County, Arizona.

Title:
Supplying & Delivering Liquid Asphalt Products No. B24-03-011
Submitted For: Madhav Mundle, Public Works Director 
Submitted By:
Cheryl Hollins, Account Specialist
Department:
Public Works
Presented By:
Madhav Mundle
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of Contract #B24-03-011 Supplying and Delivering Liquid Asphalt Products to
Hawker & Evan Asphalt Company Inc., and Cactus Asphalt, Inc.
Background:
This is an annual contract to provide the sale and delivery (as required), application, and other aspects of
liquid asphalt products used for the County's pavement preservation work in patching potholes, chip seal
application and fog seal treatments on public roads in Navajo County.
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Governmental Center,
Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 p.m. M.S.T., April 11, 2024, in strict accordance with specifications on file
in the office of the Navajo County Department of Public Works. No bids were accepted after 3:00 p.m.
M.S.T. The bids were publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15 p.m.  Navajo County may award contracts
to two bidders for each item, and select the bidder at the time of purchase based on price, convenience,
availability, and/or quality. The award of these contracts provides the County with the flexibility to utilize
a second contractor, should the lowest bidder not be available.
Staff recommends awarding contracts to Hawker & Evans Asphalt Company Inc. and Cactus Asphalt,
Inc. per the attached bid tab.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.

Y

Funds are budgeted for the FY2024/2025 improvement projects.

CONTRACT 
 
THIS CONTRACT is made and entered into as of May 14, 2024 (the “Effective 
Date”) by and between Navajo County (“County”) and Cactus Asphalt (“Contractor”). 
 
1. SCOPE OF WORK: Contractor shall furnish any and all materials, equipment and 
labor required for performing all work for Supplying & Delivering Liquid Asphalt 
Products, Contract # B24-03-011, in accordance with the Contract Documents, in a 
workmanlike manner to the satisfaction of County through its Engineers. 
 
2. CONTRACT DOCUMENTS: The Contract Documents comprise this Contract and the 
attached 
 Call for Bids, 
 Specifications, 
 General Provisions, 
 Special 
Provisions, 
Proposal dated April 11, 2024 
 Bidding Schedule, 
 Bid Bond, 
 
Performance Bond, 
 No Collusion Affidavit, 
 Subcontracting Certification, 
 Plans, 
and addenda thereto, if any.  If there is any conflict in the documents, this Contract 
shall control over all others; the Specifications shall control over the General 
Provisions and Special Provisions; and the Special Provisions shall control over the 
General Provisions. 
 
3. NO LIENS / TIME FOR COMPLETION: Contractor shall furnish all materials, 
equipment and labor in accordance with the Contract Documents free and clear of all 
claims, liens and charges whatsoever, within the time, or times, stated in the Proposal. 
 
4. CANCELLATION: This Contract is subject to cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-
511 (concerning conflicts of interest). 
 
5. PAYMENTS:  Payment shall be made on the basis of the unit price(s) bid for the 
awarded items in the Bidding Schedule for Contract #B24-03-011.  Contractor shall 
submit invoices to County for all work performed hereunder.  No later than 30 days 
after each invoice has been verified and approved by the Engineer, County shall pay 
to Contractor 100% of the approved amount. 
 
6. CERTIFICATIONS: 
 
A. PROHIBITION OF BOYCOTT OF ISRAEL:  Contractor certifies that neither it 
nor its subcontractors are currently or will be engaged in, for the duration of this 
Contract engage in, a boycott of Israel as defined by A.R.S. § 35-393 et seq.  
Violation of this certification may result in an action by the County up to and 
including termination of the Contract. 
 
B. WRITTEN CERTIFICATION PURSUANT TO A.R.S. §35-394:  Pursuant to 
A.R.S. § 35-394, Contractor certifies that it is not currently using, and agrees 
for the duration of this Agreement to not use (1) the forced labor of ethnic 
Uyghurs in the People’s Republic of China; (2) any goods or services produced 
by the  C-2 forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs in the People’s Republic of China; 
and (3) any contractors, subcontractors, or suppliers that use the forced labor

<!-- page 345 -->
C-2 
of ethnic Uyghurs in the People’s Republic of China.  The Subrecipient further 
acknowledges and agrees that (1) if the Subrecipient becomes aware during 
the term of this Agreement that it is not in compliance with this certification that 
the Subrecipient will notify the County within five (5) business days after 
becoming aware of the noncompliance; and (2) if the Subrecipient does not 
provide the County with a written certification that the Subrecipient has 
remedied the noncompliance within one hundred eighty (180) after giving 
notice thereof, the same shall constitute a material breach of this Agreement, 
which shall then terminate automatically, except that if the Agreement 
termination date occurs before the end of the remedy period, this Agreement 
terminates on the Agreement’s termination date.  The County retains the legal 
right to inspect the records of the Contractor and all Subcontractors to ensure 
compliance with this certification for the duration of this Agreement. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the authorized representatives of the parties have 
executed this Contract as of the Effective Date.

Title:
Attachment C to the Navajo Nation Intergovernmental Agreement
Submitted For: Madhav Mundle, Public Works Director 
Submitted By:
Alexandria Porras, Administrative Specialist
Department:
Public Works
Presented By:
Madhav Mundle
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of  Attachment C to The Navajo Nation Intergovernmental Agreement for assistance with
roadway maintenance materials for the N8065 Road Maintenance Project.
Background:
The Forest Lake Chapter, in coordination with the Navajo Division of Transportation, has requested
assistance for roadway maintenance material for the N8065 Road Maintenance Project. N8065 is an
unpaved roadway which provides access to the Black Mesa Chapter House, businesses, schools and N41
for residents living northeast of N41 and parallel to Oraibi Wash. The Scope Of Work is for aggregate
base gravel placement starting at the beginning of the project, which is 1.70 miles east of the intersection
of N41 and N8065. The project will place aggregate base gravel material along N8065 for approximately
O.77 miles in Forest Lake, AZ.  The District I Special Road Funds/Navajo Nation Funds will be used to
purchase the required materials for this project.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year:
2024-2025
Budgeted Y/N:
Y
Amount Requested: 99,974.55
Fiscal Impact:
Funds are budgeted under District I Special Road Funds.

ATTACHMENT C
To the Intergovernmental Agreement Between
THE NAVAJO NATION
AND
NAVAJO COUNTY
The ATTACHMENT C is approved pursuant to the Intergovernmental Agreement (IGA) between
the Navajo Nation and Navajo County, effective date of April 3, 2024.  The IGA was assigned
Contract No. _______________.  This ATTACHMENT C is hereby approved by the signatures
below in accordance with Paragraph 1.1 Initial and Additional PROJECTS of the IGA between the
Navajo Nation and Navajo County (Contract No. ____________) and provides for the following:
SCOPE OF WORK:
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors, by motion duly passed at the Board's regular meeting
on May 14, 2024 has approved the expenditure of up to $99,974.55 in District I Special Project
Road Funds to be used for the purchase of AB Gravel Material for N8065 Road Maintenance
Project. Such material shall be used for road maintenance activities which are located on the
Navajo Reservation, Forest Lake Chapter, in Navajo County, within Supervisorial District No. I of
the County.
For any material acquired in whole or in part with the Allocated Funds, Navajo County shall
have no responsibility or liability whatsoever in regard to such material. The Nation shall be
responsible for identifying, prioritizing, scheduling, and performing all maintenance activities
for which the Allocated Funds are used in whole or in part.  It is understood that the County's
sole obligation hereunder is to make payment of the Allocated Funds.
This Attachment shall be valid effective April 23, 2024 through June 30, 2025.  Any Allocated
Funds that are unused as of June 30, 2025 shall no longer be available for distribution pursuant
to this ATTACHMENT C.
The material purchased by the County with the allocated funds shall be delivered to the Navajo
Nation, Pinon Chapter at a time and location to be determined:
The request for funds will be sent to:

Board of Supervisors Regular
7. c. 1.        
Meeting Date: 05/14/2024
 
Title:
TXT-A 24-005 Institutional Residences and Group Homes
Submitted By: Cody Cooper, Planning Manager
Department:
Public Works
Division:
Planning & Development
Presented By: Cody Cooper
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 17-2024,
approving/denying a text amendment to Article 20, Section 2001 and Article 25, Section 2518 of the
Navajo County Zoning Ordinance, regarding Institutional Residences and other Group Homes which do
not qualify as Group Homes for the Disabled. 
Background:
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff have determined that a need exists to
amend the current scope of the provisions within the Zoning Ordinance for Institutional
Residences and Group Homes which do not qualify as Group Homes for the Disabled, to clarify
the requirements and opportunities for developing these higher-density special uses. Currently,
the Zoning Ordinance only mentions that Institutional Residences and Group Homes are a
special use permittable upon obtaining a Special Use Permit but does not provide any further
requirements regarding appropriate zoning districts or development standards. The proposed
amendments supply further information on these important topics.
Based upon the above, Staff is recommending that the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance be
amended within Article 20, Section 2001, to provide information about which zoning districts are
eligible for the development of Institutional Residences and other Group Homes which do not
qualify as Group Homes for the Disabled, and to provide clerical reference to Article 25, Section
2518, which would be amended to include all development standards and expectations for
those projects.
Within the attachments to this Staff report, the Planning and Zoning Commission will find a track
changes and a clean copy version of the amendments, as currently proposed by Staff.
At the Thursday, March 21st, 2024 Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission Hearing,
the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously recommended approval of
this Text Amendment to the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, as presented by Staff.
Planning and Development Services Staff have received no public comments for the text
amendment as of April 30th, 2024.
 
Recommendation:

<!-- page 361 -->
The Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission and Navajo County Planning and Development
Services Staff recommend approval of the Text Amendment, as proposed within the track changes and
clean copies attached in the Staff Report.

NAVAJO N
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT
PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT SERVICES
STAFF REPORT
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Hearing Date:
May 14, 2024
Case No.:
TXT-A 24-005
Action: 
Zoning Ordinance Text Amendment
Project Name:
Amendment to Articles 20 and 25 of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance
– Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Request Description: Consideration of a text amendment to Article 20, Section 2001 and Article 25,
Section 2518 of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance, regarding Institutional Residences and other
Group Homes which do not qualify as Group Homes for the Disabled.
Recommendation: Staff recommends approval of the Text Amendment, as shown in the track
changes and clean copies attached.  The Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission also
unanimously recommended approval of this Text Amendment.
BACKGROUND/ANALYSIS
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff have determined that a need exists to amend
the current scope of the provisions within the Zoning Ordinance for Institutional Residences and Group
Homes which do not qualify as Group Homes for the Disabled, to clarify the requirements and
opportunities for developing these higher-density special uses. Currently, the Zoning Ordinance only
mentions that Institutional Residences and Group Homes are a special use permittable upon obtaining a
Special Use Permit but does not provide any further requirements regarding appropriate zoning districts
or development standards. The proposed amendments supply further information on these important
topics.
Based upon the above, Staff is recommending that the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance be amended
within Article 20, Section 2001, to provide information about which zoning districts are eligible for the
development of Institutional Residences and other Group Homes which do not qualify as Group Homes
for the Disabled, and to provide clerical reference to Article 25, Section 2518, which would be amended
to include all development standards and expectations for those projects.
Within the attachments to this Staff report, the Planning and Zoning Commission will find a track
changes and a clean copy version of the amendments, as currently proposed by Staff.

<!-- page 363 -->

Attachment 1 – Track Changes
Article 20 is hereby amended as follows (new text is shown in underline, and text proposed to be deleted is
shown as strikeout):
Section 2001 – Special Uses.
18. Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes within the R-2, R-3, and C-R zoning districts, subject

Article 25 is hereby amended as follows (new text is shown in underline, and text proposed to be deleted is
shown as strikeout):
Section 2518 – Group Homes for the Disabled
Group Homes for the Disabled
1. Purpose.
The purpose of these regulations is to permit disabled persons, as defined by state and federal law, to
reside in single-family residences in compliance with the Fair Housing Act and Arizona state law, while
maintaining the residential character of neighborhoods.
2. Applicability.
a.
Zoning Confirmation: Prior to registration, requests for zoning confirmation may be submitted to
the Planning and Zoning Department to confirm the proposed location of the group home is
permitted per this Section.
b. Additional Requirements of State Law: Notwithstanding the foregoing, if the State has adopted
laws or rules for the regulation of a specific type of home, such as a Group Home for the
Developmentally Disabled or an Assisted Living Home as defined by state statutes, then any
such State law or rule shall apply in addition to the conditions listed herein and shall preempt any
conflicting condition listed herein.
3. Performance Standards.
Group Homes for the Disabled shall be located, developed, and operated in compliance with the
following standards:
a.
Separation: The minimum separation between group homes shall be 1,200 feet as prescribed by
A.R.S. § 36-582, as measured by the closest property lines.
b. Occupancy: The number of residents, excluding staff, shall not exceed 10.
c.
Exterior Appearance: There shall be no sign or other exterior indication of a group home visible

<!-- page 365 -->
Board of Supervisors
          
P a g e
4 | 
from a street.
d. Compliance with Building, Environmental Health, and Fire Safety Regulations: Group homes
shall be required to comply with all relevant building, environmental health, and fire safety
regulations.  If a group home has one or more non-ambulatory residents, building code
requirements in addition to those applicable to group homes containing no non-ambulatory
residents, shall apply.
e.
Licensing: Group homes shall comply with applicable licensing requirements.
f.
Parking: Any parking for the group homes shall be on site.
4. Permits and Administration.
a.
Property is considered a Group Home for the Disabled under this Ordinance after the Navajo
County Planning and Zoning Department issues a Permit for a Group Home for the Disabled for
that property.  Only properties serving occupants who fit the definition of disabled under the Fair
Housing Act are eligible for determination as a Group Home for the Disabled.
b. In order to secure a permit for a group home for the disabled, an application must be submitted
on the form prescribed by the Planning and Zoning Department.  The application must include
the following:
(1) A site plan
(2) A notarized statement detailing qualifications
(3) Copies of applicable licenses
(4) Affidavit of compliance and other documentation indicating the property as a group home
for the disabled
(5) Any additional information or plans, if deemed necessary
c.
If standards are not met, the home shall be considered another group home or institutional
residential use and shall be subject to the provisions of the Zoning Ordinance guiding such
establishments.  Status as a Group Home for the Disabled is open to review by the Navajo
County Planning and Zoning Department and may be cancelled at any time upon a determination
by the Director that the property is no longer being used for a qualifying purpose or meeting
standards laid forth in this Ordinance.
Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes
1. Purpose: The purpose of these regulations is to provide guidelines for permitting group homes which
do not qualify as group homes for the disabled.
2. Applicability:

<!-- page 366 -->
Board of Supervisors
          
P a g e
5 | 
a.
Notwithstanding the foregoing, if the State has adopted laws or rules for the regulation of a
specific type of group home as defined by State statutes, then any such State law or rule shall
apply in addition to the conditions listed herein and shall preempt any conflicting condition
listed herein.
3. Performance Standards: Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes shall be located,
developed, and operated in compliance with the following standards:
a.
Separation: The minimum separation between group homes shall be 1200 feet as measured
by the closest property lines. These group homes shall also not be located within 1200 feet
from any religious, educational, or childcare facilities.
b. Zoning: Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes shall only be allowed in the R-2, R
-3, and C-R zoning districts upon acquiring a Special Use Permit.
c.
Occupancy: Occupancy shall be regulated within the zoning districts as listed below:
i. R-2 and R-3: One resident for every bedroom within the residence.
ii. C-R: Up to two residents for every bedroom within the facility.
d. Exterior Appearance: Within R-2 and R-3 zoning districts, there shall be no sign or exterior
indication of a group home visible from the street. Within C-R zoning, signage and exterior
shall comply with the regulations of the zoning district. For group homes within the R-2 and
R-3 zoning districts, a solid wall or fence acceptable to the Director of Planning and
Development Services shall be required around any side and rear yards.
e.
Compliance with Building, Environmental Health, and Fire Safety Regulations: Group homes
shall be required to comply with all relevant building, environmental health, and fire safety
regulations. If a group home has one or more non-ambulatory residents, building code
requirements in addition to those applicable to group homes containing no non-ambulatory
residents, shall apply.
f.
Licensing: Group Homes shall comply with applicable licensing requirements.
g. Parking: Any parking for the group homes shall be on site and comply with all parking
standards. No parking will be allowed on any street or within any right-of-way.
4. Permits and Administration:
a.
Property is considered an Institutional Residence or Other Group Home if it is a facility for
living, sleeping, and sanitation and may include facilities for eating and cooking for
occupancy other than a family which does not qualify as a group home for the disabled.
b. In order for an Institutional Residence or Other Group Home to be an allowed use, a Special
Use Permit must be obtained. The application must include the following:
i. A site plan

<!-- page 367 -->

ii. A copy of the recorded deed or sales contract

4. Compatibility of the proposed use with adjacent uses and character of the area
5. Details of the operation (number of residents to be served, number of
individuals employed on-site, rules and regulations for operation and for
residents, resident/facility schedules, security procedures, etc.)
iv. A notarized statement detailing qualifications
v. Copies of applicable licenses

RESOLUTION ____-24
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS,
APPROVING AMENDMENTS TO THE NAVAJO COUNTY ZONING ORDINANCE
WHEREAS, Planning & Development Staff has recommended the amendment
of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance to Articles 20 and 25; and
WHEREAS, a duly noticed public hearing on said amendments was held by the
Planning & Zoning Commission on March 21, 2024; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, after considering the
testimony and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of
Staff, has found that the Zoning Ordinance amendments are consistent with the public
health, safety, and general welfare and should be approved as set forth herein; and
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the Navajo County Board
of Supervisors that Articles 20 and 25 are amended within the Navajo County Zoning
Ordinance as follows:
SECTION 1. Article 20 is hereby amended as follows:
Section 2001 – Special Uses.
18. Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes within the R-2, R-3, and C-R zoning
districts, subject to the provisions of Section 2518.
SECTION 2. Article 25 is hereby amended as follows:
Section 2518 – Group Homes
Group Homes for the Disabled
1. Purpose.
The purpose of these regulations is to permit disabled persons, as defined by state and federal
law, to reside in single-family residences in compliance with the Fair Housing Act and
Arizona state law, while maintaining the residential character of neighborhoods.
2. Applicability.

<!-- page 373 -->
a.
Zoning Confirmation: Prior to registration, requests for zoning confirmation may be
submitted to the Planning and Zoning Department to confirm the proposed location of
the group home is permitted per this Section.
b. Additional Requirements of State Law: Notwithstanding the foregoing, if the State
has adopted laws or rules for the regulation of a specific type of home, such as a
Group Home for the Developmentally Disabled or an Assisted Living Home as
defined by state statutes, then any such State law or rule shall apply in addition to the
conditions listed herein and shall preempt any conflicting condition listed herein.
3. Performance Standards.
Group Homes for the Disabled shall be located, developed, and operated in compliance with
the following standards:
a.
Separation: The minimum separation between group homes shall be 1,200 feet as
prescribed by A.R.S. § 36-582, as measured by the closest property lines.
b. Occupancy: The number of residents, excluding staff, shall not exceed 10.
c.
Exterior Appearance: There shall be no sign or other exterior indication of a group
home visible from a street.
d. Compliance with Building, Environmental Health, and Fire Safety Regulations:
Group homes shall be required to comply with all relevant building, environmental
health, and fire safety regulations.  If a group home has one or more non-ambulatory
residents, building code requirements in addition to those applicable to group homes
containing no non-ambulatory residents, shall apply.
e.
Licensing: Group homes shall comply with applicable licensing requirements.
f.
Parking: Any parking for the group homes shall be on site.
4. Permits and Administration.
a.
Property is considered a Group Home for the Disabled under this Ordinance after the
Navajo County Planning and Zoning Department issues a Permit for a Group Home
for the Disabled for that property.  Only properties serving occupants who fit the
definition of disabled under the Fair Housing Act are eligible for determination as a
Group Home for the Disabled.
b. In order to secure a permit for a group home for the disabled, an application must be
submitted on the form prescribed by the Planning and Zoning Department.  The
application must include the following:
(1) A site plan

<!-- page 374 -->
(2) A notarized statement detailing qualifications
(3) Copies of applicable licenses
(4) Affidavit of compliance and other documentation indicating the property as a
group home for the disabled
(5) Any additional information or plans, if deemed necessary
c.
If standards are not met, the home shall be considered another group home or
institutional residential use and shall be subject to the provisions of the Zoning
Ordinance guiding such establishments.  Status as a Group Home for the Disabled is
open to review by the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Department and may be
cancelled at any time upon a determination by the Director that the property is no
longer being used for a qualifying purpose or meeting standards laid forth in this
Ordinance.
Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes
1. Purpose: The purpose of these regulations is to provide guidelines for permitting group
homes which do not qualify as group homes for the disabled.
2. Applicability:
a.
Notwithstanding the foregoing, if the State has adopted laws or rules for the
regulation of a specific type of group home as defined by State statutes, then any
such State law or rule shall apply in addition to the conditions listed herein and
shall preempt any conflicting condition listed herein.
3. Performance Standards: Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes shall be
located, developed, and operated in compliance with the following standards:
a.
Separation: The minimum separation between group homes shall be 1200 feet as
measured by the closest property lines. These group homes shall also not be
located within 1200 feet from any religious, educational, or childcare facilities.
b. Zoning: Institutional Residences and Other Group Homes shall only be allowed in
the R-2, R-3, and C-R zoning districts upon acquiring a Special Use Permit.
c.
Occupancy: Occupancy shall be regulated within the zoning districts as listed
below:
i. R-2 and R-3: One resident for every bedroom within the residence.
ii. C-R: Up to two residents for every bedroom within the facility.

<!-- page 375 -->
d. Exterior Appearance: Within R-2 and R-3 zoning districts, there shall be no sign
or exterior indication of a group home visible from the street. Within C-R zoning,
signage and exterior shall comply with the regulations of the zoning district. For
group homes within the R-2 and R-3 zoning districts, a solid wall or fence
acceptable to the Director of Planning and Development Services shall be
required around any side and rear yards.
e.
Compliance with Building, Environmental Health, and Fire Safety Regulations:
Group homes shall be required to comply with all relevant building,
environmental health, and fire safety regulations. If a group home has one or more
non-ambulatory residents, building code requirements in addition to those
applicable to group homes containing no non-ambulatory residents, shall apply.
f.
Licensing: Group Homes shall comply with applicable licensing requirements.
g. Parking: Any parking for the group homes shall be on site and comply with all
parking standards. No parking will be allowed on any street or within any right-of
-way.
4. Permits and Administration:
a.
Property is considered an Institutional Residence or Other Group Home if it is a
facility for living, sleeping, and sanitation and may include facilities for eating
and cooking for occupancy other than a family which does not qualify as a group
home for the disabled.
b. In order for an Institutional Residence or Other Group Home to be an allowed
use, a Special Use Permit must be obtained. The application must include the
following:
i. A site plan
ii. A copy of the recorded deed or sales contract
iii. A project narrative, including:
1. Benefits of the use to the area
2. An explanation of the proposed use
3. A schedule for development
4. Compatibility of the proposed use with adjacent uses and character
of the area
5. Details of the operation (number of residents to be served, number
of individuals employed on-site, rules and regulations for operation
and for residents, resident/facility schedules, security procedures,
etc.)

<!-- page 376 -->
iv. A notarized statement detailing qualifications
v. Copies of applicable licenses
vi. Any additional information or plans, if deemed necessary
SECTION 3. Severability.
If any section, subsection, sentence, clause, phrase or portion of this ordinance or any part
of the code adopted herein by reference is for any reason held to be invalid or
unconstitutional by the decision of any court of competent jurisdiction, such decision shall
not affect the validity of the remaining portions thereof.
SECTION 4. Clerical Corrections
The County Clerk is hereby authorized to correct typographical and grammatical errors, as
well as errors of wording and punctuation, as necessary related to this ordinance as
amended herein, and to make formatting changes needed for purposes of clarity and form,
or consistency, within thirty (30) days following adoption by the Board of Supervisors.
SECTION 5. Effective Date
This ordinance shall become effective thirty (30) days following adoption by the Board of
Supervisors.

Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without District’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed 
on Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 8, 
9, 10, 12, 15 and 18.  Consultant is responsible for full performance of this 
Agreement regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
5. 
Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care 
and the orderly progress of the Project, and in accordance with an agreed upon 
schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a 
schedule approved by District shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by 
District or Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if necessary, as the Project 
proceeds.  The date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days 
that performance is delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other 
causes beyond Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the 
parties’ representatives. 
 
6. 
Payment.  District shall pay to Consultant a lump sum of two hundred eighty-eight 
thousand three hundred ninety dollars ($288,390.00) as payment in full for all 
services rendered by Consultant pursuant to this Agreement.  Payments shall be made 
within 30 days of District’s receipt of Consultant’s monthly invoices.  Each invoice 
shall detail the work performed during the billing period.  Invoice amounts in percent 
of Project cost shall not exceed the percentage of completion of the Project as 
approved by District. 
 
7. 
Defects in Work.  District may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifications.  Consultant agrees to promptly 
remedy all such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
controversy or claim through informal negotiation as set forth in Section 12.  No 
compensation shall be paid for any rejected work until such issues have been 
resolved.

representatives, officers, officials and employees against such losses as set 
forth below.  Consultant shall provide District with a current Certificate of 
Insurance naming District as an additional insured (except for Errors and 
Omissions / Professional Liability coverage).

and expenses (including but not limited to reasonable attorney fees, court costs, and

Records.  Consultant shall retain, and shall require each subcontractor to retain, all

years after completion of the work.  All such documents shall be subject to inspection

copy of any or all such documents shall be produced by Consultant at the request and

11. 
Non-Assignment.  Consultant shall not assign any right or interest in this Agreement 
without District’s prior written approval, nor shall Consultant delegate any duty 
hereunder without District’s prior written approval. 
 
12. 
Negotiation of Disputes.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
claim or controversy or claim through informal negotiation.  Notice of any claim or 
controversy shall be provided in writing, with supporting documentation, to the 
recipient designated in Section 21.  The recipient shall have seven calendar days to 
prepare and deliver a written response.  If the parties fail to resolve the disputes 
within a reasonable period of not less than ten days, either party may pursue available 
legal remedies. 
 
13. 
Suspension and Termination by District: 
 
A. Suspension.  District may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to suspend, 
delay or interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment shall be made 
to the completion date and for any increase in the cost of performance resulting 
from the suspension, delay or interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the 
extent that performance is or would have been suspended, delayed or interrupted 
by another cause for which Consultant is responsible. 
 
B. Termination for Cause.  District may terminate this Agreement for cause if 
Consultant refuses or fails to supply enough properly skilled workers to perform 
this Agreement; fails to make required payments to subcontractors; disregards 
laws, ordinances, rules, regulations or orders of a public authority having 
jurisdiction; or otherwise commits a material breach of this Agreement.  When 
any of the foregoing causes exist, District shall give Consultant written notice and 
a ten-day opportunity to cure the default.  If the default remains uncured, District 
may (without prejudice to any other rights or remedies it may have) terminate this 
Agreement and finish the work or cause it to be finished.  If the unpaid balance of 
the Agreement exceeds the cost of finishing the work (including any expenses 
incurred by District as a result of Consultant’s default), Consultant shall be 
entitled to payment for its performance up to the amount of such excess.  If the 
cost of finishing the work exceeds the unpaid balance, Consultant shall pay the 
difference to District. 
 
C. Termination for Convenience.  District, by written notice to Consultant, may 
terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of District it 
is in District’s best interests to do so.  Consultant shall be paid for all material, 
equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses and 
a reasonable allowance for profit and overhead, provided that such payments, 
exclusive of termination expenses, shall not exceed the total amount payable 
pursuant to Section 6 less any payments previously made to Consultant.  
Consultant shall not be entitled to profit and overhead on material, equipment and 
services which were not provided.

Sky Engineering & Survey LLC (Painted Sky). HDR will assist Navajo County (NC) in coordinating 
this task order and managing the contract. 
Aerotech Mapping and Painted Sky will be responsible for performing the work and the quality control 
as requested by Navajo County.  
SCOPE OF WORK 
Task 1.0 Project Management:  This work includes monitoring the budget, overseeing project 
progress, establishing the project execution timeline, coordinating scope of works, and 
submitting monthly status reports and invoices to the NC Public Works Department Project 
Manager. 
Task 2.0 Meetings:  This work involves attending meetings, provide meetings agendas and 
document meeting minutes. The Kick-Off-Meeting (KOM) will start the project and discuss the 
project approach and timeline. It is assumed all meetings will be conducted virtually and no in-
person meetings are required. 
Task 3.0 Aerial Mapping:  This task will be performed and completed by Aerotech Mapping. 
Included as an attachment their Scope of Work and estimate. 
Task 4.0 Field Survey:  This task will be performed and completed by Painted Sky. Included as an 
attachment their Scope of Work and estimate.

Scope of Work 
Painted Sky Engineering & Survey, LLC shall provide the Services and Deliverable(s) as follows: 
Task 1 – Research Existing Mapping and Control 
Painted Sky Engineering & Survey, LLC (PSES) shall work in conjunction with AeroTech Mapping to provide 
a mapping survey for the Winslow Levee project. The mapping for the 5-ft contour interval will cover 
approximately 14,800 acres and the mapping for the 1-ft contour interval will cover approximately 2,500 
acres (most of the 2,500-acre area is located within the 14,800-acre area). The approximate boundary of 
these areas is shown in Figure 1. PSES will research existing mapping and control for the area. This first task 
allows for the preparation of the fieldwork. 
 
Task 2 – Establish Primary Control 
PSES shall establish primary control throughout the site. The primary control will include an existing NGS 
monument at the south end of the project and 2 new monuments to be set by PSES. One of these new 
monuments will be near the middle of the project and the other will be near the North end of the project. 
The new monuments will be brass caps attached to rebar set in concrete. 
 
Task 3 – Establish Local Project Control 
PSES shall establish local project control that will be tied into the primary control points. This includes the 
setting of approximately 16 new monuments. These will also be brass caps attached to rebar set in 
concrete. The local project control will also include county monuments that have been used in the past. 
PSES may include some of those monuments, but we will establish new horizontal and vertical coordinates 
for them.  
 
Task 4 – U-SMART Control Sheets 
PSES shall provide a spreadsheet containing information regarding the primary and local project control.  
The spreadsheet will include the Station Name, Latitude, Longitude, North Coordinate, East Coordinate, 
Units of Measure (International Feet), Horizontal Datum (NAD83, Zone (GeoID 18 - Arizona East 201), H-
EPOCH (2010), Elevation, and Vertical Datum (NAVD88).  PSES shall also provide a scale factor to tie 
between grid and ground coordinates for the project.  PSES shall also provide two photos (Horizon and 
Close-Up) of each control monument as required for the completion of the U-SMART form. 
 
Task 5 – 1 Foot Aerial Mapping (2,500 Acres) 
PSES shall set and survey approximately 36 aerial control panels throughout the 1-ft contour interval area. 
PSES will also provide supplemental surveying for various infrastructure throughout the site including 
fences/walls, drain inlet and outlet structures, 4 bridge crossings, and the ground in vegetated areas. PSES 
shall provide geometry and mapping for the bridge crossings. PSES shall also provide supplemental 
labeling of the area, not including the labeling of residential and commercial areas.  PSES shall also 
include control information for the mapping panels in the same format as the primary and local control.

<!-- page 399 -->
P:\M Drive Marketing\Arizona PY-SL\2024 PROPOSALS\028\Winslow Levee Aerial Mapping Survey PSA Revised.docx 
 
Page 3 of 8 
 
 
 
Task 6 – 5 Foot Aerial Mapping (14,800 Acres) 
PSES shall set and survey approximately 11 aerial control panels throughout the 5-ft contour interval area. 
PSES will also provide a quality control plan per the SOW requirements and the sample provided, 
spreadsheets for primary and local control, a control map on mylar, supplemental labeling (excluding 
residential and commercial areas), and quality control check points.  PSES shall also include control 
information for the mapping panels in the same format as the primary and local control. 
 
Task 7 – Quality Control Evaluation 
PSES shall send a different survey crew with a different set of GPS equipment out to the site for a quality 
control evaluation. This will ensure that the coordinates for the control network are correct and that 
outside parties will be able to tie into the network. 
 
Task 8 – Meetings 
This task accounts for any meetings that PSES may need to attend for the project. 
 
Task 9 – Project Management 
This task accounts for all communication with Navajo County, AeroTech, and HDR as well as 
interdepartmental communication throughout the duration of the project. 
 
PSES proposes to provide these services on a Lump Sum basis for a fee of $143,750.00. These services will 
be invoiced monthly, based on the amount of work that is completed each month. 
 
Exclusions: 
Items that are specifically excluded from this proposal and if required, can be provided at an additional 
fee are the following: 
• 
Record of Surveys for Right of Ways 
• 
Construction Staking

<!-- page 405 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular
7. d. 2. a.        
Meeting Date:
05/14/2024
 
Title:
Intergovernmental Agreement for the Silver Creek Flood Control District Millet Swale
Drainage Improvement Project in District III (Jason Whiting)
Submitted For: William Flake, Assistant County Engineer 
Submitted By:
Donald Perkins, Flood Control Manager
Department:
Public Works
Presented By:
Donald Perkins
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of an Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County Flood Control District and
Silver Creek Flood Control District for financial assistance for the Millet Swale Drainage Improvement
Project in the amount of $100,000.
Background:
The Silver Creek Flood Control District (SCFD) is requesting financial assistance with their Value
Engineering of the Millet Swale Drainage Improvement Project.  The purpose of this IGA is to authorize
the County to partner financially, as the Navajo County Flood Control District and the SCFD share a
common interest to improve Millet Swale drainage areas to help reduce the chance of future flooding. The
District is requesting financial assistance from Navajo County Flood Control District to reimburse the
SCFD based on paid invoices to the Engineer. Payment to the District will be consistent with progress and
completion of the Engineering Design.  The District shall be solely responsible for the design,
procurement, permitting, and construction of the Project. The SCFD understands and agrees that the
County’s sole participation and obligation with respect to the Project shall be to provide financial
assistance in the amount not to exceed $100,000.  Public Works has reviewed the request and finds it
consistent with the purpose of the Flood Control District and supports the Project.  
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends the execution of this IGA
